L2043: ## Reviewer's Comment
L2044: The Applicant's proposal for MIC breakpoints for Enterobacteriaceae, *P. aeruginosa*, and anaerobes (agar dilution) matched the breakpoints accepted by FDA and by CLSI for imipenem, however, for Enterobacteriaceae and *P. aeruginosa*, the disk criteria were different. (b) (4)
L2045: (b) (4) See clinical microbiology summary and recommendations for details. (b) (4)
L2046: The evidence of efficacy in the clinical trials and joint PTA assessment provide support to maintain existing imipenem broth microdilution breakpoints for IMI/REL. In addition, disk potency and broth microdilution correlation studies supported the use of 10/25 IMI/REL Kirby-Bauer disk for testing, which resulted in zone diameters that differentiate susceptible and resistant bacterial isolates.
L2047: The Applicant proposes to institute new breakpoints for Kirby-Bauer disk diffusion, employing a disk mass of 10 mcg imipenem and 25 mcg REL, based on analysis of the broth microdilution disk zone diameter correlation employing clinical and challenge isolates. The proposed disk mass of imipenem and relebactam is acceptable because it gave the best reproducibility.
L2048: ## Reviewer's Comment
L2049: The Agency's clinical pharmacology review team found their PK-PD target attainment analysis to be supportive of the breakpoints proposed by the Applicant for imipenem-relebactam. Additionally, the Agency's proposed breakpoints were most heavily based on the clinical microbiology in vitro data and reliance on the imipenem-cilastatin labeling as the clinical data
L2050: for the PN003, PN004 and PN013 trials was very limited. The favorable clinical responses shown by pathogen during imipenem-relebactam clinical trials are shown in the clinical section of the review.
L2051: ## 15.6.7 Final Clinical Microbiology Recommendations
L2052: From a clinical microbiology perspective, the information provided by the Applicant supports the efficacy of imipenem relebactam for the treatment of susceptible bacteria for the indications of cUTI and cIAI. The following is a summary of the Agency's proposed clinical microbiology labeling changes and rationale:
L2053: - Subsection 12.4 has been updated in accordance with the FDA documents titled, "Microbiology Data for Systemic Antibacterial Drugs-Development, Analysis, and Presentation: Guidance for Industry" and "Systemic Antibacterial and Antifungal Drugs: Susceptibility Test Interpretive Criteria Labeling for NDAs and ANDAs: Guidance for Industry".
- Quality Control ranges used for susceptibility testing have been accepted by the Clinical and Laboratory Standards Institute (CLSI) and are recommended here as published in the current CLSI document M100.

This scatter plot compares CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals) on the Y-axis (ranging from -4 to 6) against Population Predicted Concentration (nM) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 100000). The data points are randomly scattered around the dashed line (y=0), suggesting no systematic bias in the residuals.
Bottom Right: CWRES vs Time Since Last Dose
This scatter plot compares CWRES on the Y-axis (ranging from -4 to 6) against Time Since Last Dose (h) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 10). The data points are randomly scattered around the dashed line (y=0), suggesting no systematic bias in the residuals over time.
Source: Applicant's population pharmacokinetic analysis report; page 64 of 320.

L2104: Figure 28 displays two scatter plots showing the 24-hour change from baseline in \log_{10} CFU (Y-axis) versus Relebactam AUC0-24 (mg\cdoth/L) (top panel) and Relebactam AUC0-24/MIC (h) (bottom panel). The data points represent P. aeruginosa strains PA24226 (red circles), PA24227 (blue circles), and PA24354 (green circles). A red sigmoid curve is fitted to the data, indicating the relationship between the pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (PK/PD) metric and the bacterial killing effect.
The top panel shows the relationship between Relebactam AUC0-24 and bacterial killing. The fitted curve has an r^2 value of 0.7. The X-axis ranges from 1 to 1000 mg\cdoth/L, and the Y-axis ranges from -4 to 6 \log_{10} CFU.
The bottom panel shows the relationship between Relebactam AUC0-24/MIC and bacterial killing. The fitted curve has an r^2 value of 0.82. The X-axis ranges from 0.1 to 100 h, and the Y-axis ranges from -4 to 6 \log_{10} CFU.

L2118: Figure 41 displays two bar charts showing the percentage of patients achieving imipenem and relebactam PK targets (Y-axis, left scale) and the percentage of strains at MIC (Y-axis, right scale) versus Imipenem/Relebactam MIC \mu\text{g/mL} (X-axis) for Pseudomonas aeruginosa (left panel) and Enterobacteriaceae (right panel).
The charts compare Clinical trial distribution (red bars) and Surveillance distribution (grey bars). The X-axis shows MIC values: 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00, 2.00, 4.00, 8.00, 16.00, 32.00, 64.00 \mu\text{g/mL}.
Vertical dashed lines indicate EUCAST and CLSI Breakpoint MICs. A solid horizontal line indicates 90% PTA.
For P. aeruginosa (Left): The 90% PTA line is at 2.00 \mu\text{g/mL}. The Clinical trial distribution (red bars) shows high target attainment (around 95%) for MICs up to 0.25 \mu\text{g/mL}, dropping sharply thereafter. The Surveillance distribution (grey bars) shows high target attainment (around 85%) for MICs up to 0.50 \mu\text{g/mL}, dropping sharply thereafter.
For Enterobacteriaceae (Right): The 90% PTA line is at 1.00 \mu\text{g/mL}. The Clinical trial distribution (red bars) shows high target attainment (around 95%) for MICs up to 0.25 \mu\text{g/mL}, dropping sharply thereafter. The Surveillance distribution (grey bars) shows high target attainment (around 85%) for MICs up to 0.50 \mu\text{g/mL}, dropping sharply thereafter.
Overlaid lines (right Y-axis, % of Strains at MIC) show the distribution of MICs across different CrCL groups (15-30 mL/min to 210-250 mL/min). These lines generally follow the trend of the bar charts, showing a steep decline in the percentage of strains achieving the target as MIC increases.
